Theorem 211.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The cellular variety V defined by convergence analysis constraints admits a stratification into 216 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 118 and intersection form of rank 5.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_216(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 216. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^118(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 211.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Given a cellular algebra A over field F with optimality condition conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 248. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 425/3.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 248 is immediate from the optimality condition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the cellular structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 425. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 3. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
